     8	import pandera.pandas as pa
     9	from pandera.errors import SchemaErrors
    10	
    11	from src.models import AnalyticsData, Coercion, Customer, RejectedRow, Subscription, ValidCandidateRow
    12	
    13	CUSTOMER_COLUMNS = ["customer_id", "signup_date", "country"]
    14	SUBSCRIPTION_COLUMNS = ["customer_id", "start_date", "end_date", "plan", "monthly_price"]
    15	
    16	
    17	def customer_schema() -> pa.DataFrameSchema:
    18	    return pa.DataFrameSchema(
    19	        {
    20	            "customer_id": pa.Column(str, nullable=False),
    21	            "signup_date": pa.Column(pa.DateTime, nullable=False),
    22	            "country": pa.Column(str, nullable=True),
    23	        },
    24	        strict=True,
    25	        coerce=True,
    26	    )
    27	
    28	
    29	def subscription_schema() -> pa.DataFrameSchema:
    30	    return pa.DataFrameSchema(
    31	        {
    32	            "customer_id": pa.Column(str, nullable=False),
    33	            "start_date": pa.Column(pa.DateTime, nullable=False),
    34	            "end_date": pa.Column(pa.DateTime, nullable=True),
    35	            "plan": pa.Column(str, nullable=False),
    36	            "monthly_price": pa.Column(float, nullable=False),
    37	        },
    38	        strict=True,
    39	        coerce=True,
    40	    )
    41	
    42	
    43	def load_analytics_data(customers_path: Path, subscriptions_path: Path) -> AnalyticsData:
    44	    customer_raw = _read_raw_csv(customers_path, CUSTOMER_COLUMNS, "customers")
    45	    subscription_raw = _read_raw_csv(subscriptions_path, SUBSCRIPTION_COLUMNS, "subscriptions")
    46	
    47	    customer_validated, customer_candidates, customer_rejects = _validate_customers(customer_raw)
    48	    subscription_validated, subscription_candidates, subscription_rejects = _validate_subscriptions(
    49	        subscription_raw
    50	    )
    51	
    52	    customers, customer_business_rejects = _build_customers(customer_validated)
    53	    subscriptions, subscription_business_rejects = _build_subscriptions(
    54	        subscription_validated, {customer.customer_id for customer in customers}
    55	    )
    56	
    57	    rejected_row_keys = {
    58	        ("customers", reject.row_number) for reject in (*customer_rejects, *customer_business_rejects)
    59	    } | {
    60	        ("subscriptions", reject.row_number)
    61	        for reject in (*subscription_rejects, *subscription_business_rejects)
    62	    }
    63	    valid_candidate_rows = [
    64	        row
    65	        for row in (*customer_candidates, *subscription_candidates)
    66	        if (row.source, row.row_number) not in rejected_row_keys
    67	    ]
    68	
    69	    return AnalyticsData(
    70	        customers=customers,
    71	        subscriptions=subscriptions,
    72	        valid_candidate_rows=valid_candidate_rows,
    73	        rejected_rows=[
    74	            *customer_rejects,
    75	            *subscription_rejects,
    76	            *customer_business_rejects,
    77	            *subscription_business_rejects,
    78	        ],
    79	    )
    80	
    81	
    82	def _read_raw_csv(path: Path, expected_columns: list[str], source: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    83	    try:
    84	        df = pd.read_csv(path, dtype=str, keep_default_na=False)
    85	    except FileNotFoundError as exc:
    86	        raise ValueError(f"{source} file not found: {path}") from exc
    87	    except pd.errors.ParserError as exc:
    88	        raise ValueError(f"{source} CSV could not be parsed: {exc}") from exc
    89	
    90	    columns = list(df.columns)
    91	    if columns != expected_columns:
    92	        missing = [column for column in expected_columns if column not in columns]
    93	        extra = [column for column in columns if column not in expected_columns]
    94	        details = []
    95	        if missing:
    96	            details.append(f"missing columns: {', '.join(missing)}")
    97	        if extra:
    98	            details.append(f"extra columns: {', '.join(extra)}")
    99	        if not details:
   100	            details.append(f"expected column order: {', '.join(expected_columns)}")
   101	        raise ValueError(f"{source} CSV has invalid columns ({'; '.join(details)})")
   102	
   103	    df = df.copy()
   104	    df.index = df.index.astype(int)
   105	    return df
   106	
   107	
   108	def _validate_customers(
   109	    raw: pd.DataFrame,
   110	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, list[ValidCandidateRow], list[RejectedRow]]:
   111	    normalized, coercions = _normalize_strings(raw, CUSTOMER_COLUMNS)
   112	    return _validate_frame(
   113	        normalized,
   114	        raw,
   115	        customer_schema(),
   116	        "customers",
   117	        "customer_id",
   118	        coercions,
   119	        date_fields=("signup_date",),
   120	        numeric_fields=(),
   121	    )
   122	
   123	
   124	def _validate_subscriptions(
   125	    raw: pd.DataFrame,
   126	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, list[ValidCandidateRow], list[RejectedRow]]:
   127	    normalized, coercions = _normalize_strings(raw, SUBSCRIPTION_COLUMNS)
   128	    blank_end_dates = normalized["end_date"].eq("")
   129	    for idx in normalized.index[blank_end_dates]:
   130	        coercions.setdefault(idx, []).append(Coercion("end_date", raw.at[idx, "end_date"], None))
   131	    normalized.loc[blank_end_dates, "end_date"] = pd.NA
   132	
   133	    return _validate_frame(
   134	        normalized,
   135	        raw,
   136	        subscription_schema(),
   137	        "subscriptions",
   138	        "customer_id",
   139	        coercions,
   140	        date_fields=("start_date", "end_date"),
   141	        numeric_fields=("monthly_price",),
   142	    )
   143	
   144	
   145	def _validate_frame(
   146	    normalized: pd.DataFrame,
   147	    raw: pd.DataFrame,
   148	    schema: pa.DataFrameSchema,
   149	    source: str,
   150	    record_id_field: str,
   151	    coercions: dict[int, list[Coercion]],
   152	    date_fields: Iterable[str],
   153	    numeric_fields: Iterable[str],
   154	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, list[ValidCandidateRow], list[RejectedRow]]:
   155	    failed_indices: set[int] = set()
   156	    failure_cases = pd.DataFrame()
   157	
   158	    try:
   159	        validated = schema.validate(normalized, lazy=True)
   160	    except SchemaErrors as exc:
   161	        failure_cases = exc.failure_cases
   162	        failed_indices = {
   163	            int(index)
   164	            for index in failure_cases.get("index", pd.Series(dtype=object)).dropna().tolist()
   165	            if str(index).isdigit()
   166	        }
   167	        candidate = normalized.drop(index=list(failed_indices))
   168	        validated = schema.validate(candidate, lazy=True) if not candidate.empty else candidate
   169	
   170	    rejects = _schema_rejects(failure_cases, raw, source, record_id_field)
   171	
   172	    candidates: list[ValidCandidateRow] = []
   173	    for idx in validated.index:
   174	        row_coercions = list(coercions.get(int(idx), []))
   175	        row_coercions.extend(_type_coercions(raw.loc[idx], validated.loc[idx], date_fields, numeric_fields))
   176	        candidates.append(
   177	            ValidCandidateRow(
   178	                source=source,
   179	                row_number=_row_number(idx),
   180	                record_id=str(validated.at[idx, record_id_field]),
   181	                coerced_fields=tuple(_dedupe_coercions(row_coercions)),
   182	            )
   183	        )
   184	
   185	    return validated, candidates, rejects
   186	
   187	
   188	def _normalize_strings(
   189	    raw: pd.DataFrame, columns: list[str]
   190	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict[int, list[Coercion]]]:
   191	    normalized = raw.copy()
   192	    coercions: dict[int, list[Coercion]] = {}
   193	    for column in columns:
   194	        for idx, value in raw[column].items():
   195	            stripped = value.strip() if isinstance(value, str) else value
   196	            if stripped != value:
   197	                coercions.setdefault(int(idx), []).append(Coercion(column, value, stripped))
   198	                normalized.at[idx, column] = stripped
   199	    return normalized, coercions
   200	
   201	
   202	def _schema_rejects(
   203	    failure_cases: pd.DataFrame, raw: pd.DataFrame, source: str, record_id_field: str
   204	) -> list[RejectedRow]:
   205	    rejects: list[RejectedRow] = []
   206	    if failure_cases.empty:
   207	        return rejects
   208	
   209	    seen: set[tuple[int, str, str]] = set()
   210	    for _, failure in failure_cases.iterrows():
   211	        raw_index = failure.get("index")
   212	        if pd.isna(raw_index):
   213	            continue
   214	        idx = int(raw_index)
   215	        field = str(failure.get("column") or "<schema>")
   216	        reason = str(failure.get("failure_case"))
   217	        key = (idx, field, reason)
   218	        if key in seen:
   219	            continue
   220	        seen.add(key)
   221	        original_value = raw.at[idx, field] if field in raw.columns else None
   222	        rejects.append(
   223	            RejectedRow(
   224	                source=source,
   225	                row_number=_row_number(idx),
   226	                record_id=str(raw.at[idx, record_id_field]) if record_id_field in raw.columns else "",
   227	                field=field,
   228	                reason=_friendly_reason(field, reason),
   229	                original_value=original_value,
   230	                coerced_value=None,
   231	            )
   232	        )
   233	    return rejects
   234	
   235	
   236	def _friendly_reason(field: str, failure_case: str) -> str:
   237	    if field in {"signup_date", "start_date", "end_date"}:
   238	        return f"{field} is not a valid ISO date"
   239	    if field == "monthly_price":
   240	        return "monthly_price is not numeric"
   241	    if failure_case in {"None", "<NA>", "nan"}:
   242	        return f"{field} is required"
   243	    return f"{field} failed validation: {failure_case}"
   244	
   245	
   246	def _type_coercions(
   247	    raw_row: pd.Series,
   248	    validated_row: pd.Series,
   249	    date_fields: Iterable[str],
   250	    numeric_fields: Iterable[str],
   251	) -> list[Coercion]:
   252	    coercions: list[Coercion] = []
   253	    for field in date_fields:
   254	        original = raw_row[field]
   255	        value = validated_row[field]
   256	        coerced = None if pd.isna(value) else value.date().isoformat()
   257	        if original.strip() == "" and coerced is None:
   258	            continue
   259	        if str(original).strip() != str(coerced):
   260	            coercions.append(Coercion(field, original, coerced))

    10	
    11	def build_report(data: AnalyticsData) -> dict[str, object]:
    12	    return {
    13	        "monthly_churned_customers": monthly_churned_customers(data.subscriptions),
    14	        "monthly_mrr": monthly_mrr(data.subscriptions),
    15	        "signup_cohorts": signup_cohorts(data.customers, data.subscriptions),
    16	        "data_quality": data.data_quality_summary_dict(),
    17	    }
    18	
    19	
    20	def monthly_mrr(subscriptions: list[Subscription]) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
    21	    if not subscriptions:
    22	        return []
    23	
    24	    first_month = _month_start(min(subscription.start_date for subscription in subscriptions))
    25	    last_month = _latest_relevant_month(subscriptions)
    26	    rows: list[dict[str, object]] = []
    27	
    28	    current = first_month
    29	    while current <= last_month:
    30	        next_month = current + relativedelta(months=1)
    31	        mrr = sum(
    32	            subscription.monthly_price
    33	            for subscription in subscriptions
    34	            if _overlaps(subscription, current, next_month)
    35	        )
    36	        rows.append({"month": _month_key(current), "mrr": round(float(mrr), 2)})
    37	        current = next_month
    38	
    39	    return rows
    40	
    41	
    42	def monthly_churned_customers(subscriptions: list[Subscription]) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
    43	    churn_counts: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
    44	    by_customer: dict[str, list[Subscription]] = defaultdict(list)
    45	    for subscription in subscriptions:
    46	        by_customer[subscription.customer_id].append(subscription)
    47	
    48	    for customer_subscriptions in by_customer.values():
    49	        ordered = sorted(customer_subscriptions, key=lambda item: item.start_date)
    50	        for subscription in ordered:
    51	            if subscription.end_date is None:
    52	                continue
    53	            window_end = subscription.end_date + timedelta(days=30)
    54	            has_timely_resubscription = any(
    55	                later.start_date >= subscription.end_date and later.start_date <= window_end
    56	                for later in ordered
    57	                if later is not subscription
    58	            )
    59	            if not has_timely_resubscription:
    60	                churn_counts[_month_key(subscription.end_date)] += 1
    61	
    62	    return [
    63	        {"month": month, "churned_customers": churn_counts[month]}
    64	        for month in sorted(churn_counts)
    65	    ]
    66	
    67	
    68	def signup_cohorts(
    69	    customers: list[Customer], subscriptions: list[Subscription]
    70	) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
    71	    subscriptions_by_customer: dict[str, list[Subscription]] = defaultdict(list)
    72	    for subscription in subscriptions:
    73	        subscriptions_by_customer[subscription.customer_id].append(subscription)
    74	
    75	    cohorts: dict[str, list[Customer]] = defaultdict(list)
    76	    for customer in customers:
    77	        cohorts[_month_key(customer.signup_date)].append(customer)
    78	
    79	    rows: list[dict[str, object]] = []
    80	    for cohort_month in sorted(cohorts):
    81	        cohort_customers = cohorts[cohort_month]
    82	        retained = 0
    83	        for customer in cohort_customers:
    84	            checkpoint = customer.signup_date + relativedelta(months=3)
    85	            if any(
    86	                _active_on(subscription, checkpoint)
    87	                for subscription in subscriptions_by_customer.get(customer.customer_id, [])
    88	            ):
    89	                retained += 1
    90	
    91	        cohort_size = len(cohort_customers)
    92	        rows.append(
    93	            {
    94	                "cohort_month": cohort_month,
    95	                "cohort_size": cohort_size,
    96	                "active_after_3_months": retained,
    97	                "retention_rate_3m": round(retained / cohort_size, 4) if cohort_size else 0.0,
    98	            }
    99	        )
   100	
   101	    return rows
   102	
   103	
   104	def _overlaps(subscription: Subscription, period_start: date, period_end: date) -> bool:
   105	    subscription_end = subscription.end_date or date.max
   106	    return subscription.start_date < period_end and subscription_end > period_start
   107	
   108	
   109	def _active_on(subscription: Subscription, checkpoint: date) -> bool:
   110	    return subscription.start_date <= checkpoint and (
   111	        subscription.end_date is None or checkpoint < subscription.end_date
   112	    )
   113	
   114	
   115	def _latest_relevant_month(subscriptions: list[Subscription]) -> date:
   116	    finite_ends = [subscription.end_date for subscription in subscriptions if subscription.end_date]
   117	    open_starts = [
   118	        subscription.start_date for subscription in subscriptions if subscription.end_date is None
   119	    ]
   120	    latest = max([*finite_ends, *open_starts])
   121	    return _month_start(latest)
   122	
   123	
   124	def _month_start(value: date) -> date:
   125	    return date(value.year, value.month, 1)
   126	
   127	
   128	def _month_key(value: date) -> str:
   129	    return f"{value.year:04d}-{value.month:02d}"

    42	def log_data_quality_issues(data: AnalyticsData, log_file: Path | None) -> None:
    43	    if not data.rejected_rows:
    44	        return
    45	
    46	    logger = logging.getLogger("data_quality")
    47	    logger.handlers.clear()
    48	    logger.setLevel(logging.INFO)
    49	    logger.propagate = False
    50	
    51	    handler: logging.Handler
    52	    if log_file is None:
    53	        handler = logging.StreamHandler(sys.stderr)
    54	    else:
    55	        log_file.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    56	        handler = logging.FileHandler(log_file, mode="w")
    57

    59	    logger.addHandler(handler)
    60	
    61	    logger.info("Rejected rows: %s", len(data.rejected_rows))
    62	    for row in data.rejected_rows:
    63	        logger.info(json.dumps(row.to_dict(), sort_keys=True))
    64

   269	def _dedupe_coercions(coercions: list[Coercion]) -> list[Coercion]:
   270	    deduped: list[Coercion] = []
   271	    seen: set[tuple[str, str, str]] = set()
   272	    for coercion in coercions:
   273	        key = (coercion.field, str(coercion.original_value), str(coercion.coerced_value))
   274	        if key not in seen:
   275	            seen.add(key)
   276	            deduped.append(coercion)
   277	    return deduped

   280	def _build_customers(validated: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[list[Customer], list[RejectedRow]]:
   281	    customers: list[Customer] = []
   282	    rejects: list[RejectedRow] = []
   283	    seen_ids: set[str] = set()
   284	
   285	    for idx, row in validated.iterrows():
   286	        customer_id = str(row["customer_id"])
   287	        if customer_id in seen_ids:
   288	            rejects.append(
   289	                RejectedRow(
   290	                    source="customers",
   291	                    row_number=_row_number(idx),
   292	                    record_id=customer_id,
   293	                    field="customer_id",
   294	                    reason="duplicate customer_id; first valid occurrence is used",
   295	                    original_value=customer_id,
   296	                )
   297	            )
   298	            continue
   299	        seen_ids.add(customer_id)
   300	        customers.append(
   301	            Customer(
   302	                customer_id=customer_id,
   303	                signup_date=row["signup_date"].date(),
   304	                country=str(row["country"]),
   305	                row_number=_row_number(idx),
   306	            )
   307	        )

   310	
   311	def _build_subscriptions(
   312	    validated: pd.DataFrame, known_customer_ids: set[str]
   313	) -> tuple[list[Subscription], list[RejectedRow]]:
   314	    subscriptions: list[Subscription] = []
   315	    rejects: list[RejectedRow] = []
   316	
   317	    for idx, row in validated.iterrows():
   318	        customer_id = str(row["customer_id"])
   319	        start_date = row["start_date"].date()
   320	        end_date = None if pd.isna(row["end_date"]) else row["end_date"].date()
   321	
   322	        if customer_id not in known_customer_ids:
   323	            rejects.append(
   324	                RejectedRow(
   325	                    source="subscriptions",
   326	                    row_number=_row_number(idx),
   327	                    record_id=customer_id,
   328	                    field="customer_id",
   329	                    reason="unknown customer_id",
   330	                    original_value=customer_id,
   331	                )
   332	            )
   333	            continue
   334	        if end_date is not None and end_date <= start_date:
   335	            rejects.append(
   336	                RejectedRow(
   337	                    source="subscriptions",
   338	                    row_number=_row_number(idx),
   339	                    record_id=customer_id,
   340	                    field="end_date",
   341	                    reason="end_date must be after start_date for half-open intervals",
   342	                    original_value=end_date.isoformat(),
   343	                    coerced_value=end_date.isoformat(),
   344	                )
   345	            )
   346	            continue
   347	
   348	        subscriptions.append(
   349	            Subscription(
   350	                customer_id=customer_id,
   351	                start_date=start_date,
   352	                end_date=end_date,
   353	                plan=str(row["plan"]),
   354	                monthly_price=float(row["monthly_price"]),
   355	                row_number=_row_number(idx),

You’re right: C014 does **not** overlap under the half-open interval rule `[start_date, end_date)`.


```text
next.start_date < previous.end_date
```

User2026-06-04T09:20:27.890Z
okay so take the C004 case for example. Remember the assumption: "Assume one active subscription per customer at a time, but customers may churn and later re-subscribe." so it needs to count as 1 instead of 2. So if there is overlap, smartly merge those lines so that you end up with a single start and end date that you can then count. This can be done if the price is the same, if the price is different, the line with the new price is a new line and the old line's end date needs to be adjusted. Does is still make sense to keep end_data to be exclusive?
Assistant2026-06-04T09:20:37.896Z
Yes, it still makes sense to keep `end_date` exclusive. It actually makes this cleanup easier and less ambiguous.

For overlap normalization, the rules would be:

1. Same customer, same price, overlapping or touching intervals:
   Merge into one interval.
   ```text
   C004 2024-02-05 -> 2024-04-01 price 55
   C004 2024-03-15 -> 2024-05-01 price 55

   becomes:
   C004 2024-02-05 -> 2024-05-01 price 55
   ```

2. Same customer, different price, overlapping intervals:
   Treat the later start as a price change. Adjust the old interval to end at the new start.
   ```text
   C123 2024-01-01 -> 2024-04-01 price 30
   C123 2024-03-15 -> 2024-06-01 price 50

   becomes:
   C123 2024-01-01 -> 2024-03-15 price 30
   C123 2024-03-15 -> 2024-06-01 price 50
   ```

With exclusive `end_date`, that adjusted old line is clean: active until the day before `2024-03-15`, and the new price starts exactly on `2024-03-15`. No double-counting and no off-by-one date problems.

So the better approach is not to reject overlaps. Normalize them into non-overlapping subscription intervals before metrics run, and log that normalization as a data-quality/coercion event.

+def _normalize_subscription_overlaps(
+    subscriptions: list[Subscription],
+) -> tuple[list[Subscription], list[AdjustedRow]]:
+    normalized: list[Subscription] = []
+    adjustments: list[AdjustedRow] = []
+
+    by_customer: dict[str, list[Subscription]] = {}
+    for subscription in subscriptions:
+        by_customer.setdefault(subscription.customer_id, []).append(subscription)
+
+    for customer_id in sorted(by_customer):
+        customer_subscriptions = sorted(
+            by_customer[customer_id], key=lambda item: (item.start_date, item.row_number)
+        )
+        customer_normalized: list[Subscription] = []
+
+        for subscription in customer_subscriptions:
+            current = subscription
+            while customer_normalized and _overlaps(customer_normalized[-1], current):
+                previous = customer_normalized[-1]
+                if previous.monthly_price == current.monthly_price:
+                    merged_end = _max_end(previous.end_date, current.end_date)
+                    customer_normalized[-1] = Subscription(
+                        customer_id=previous.customer_id,
+                        start_date=previous.start_date,
+                        end_date=merged_end,
+                        plan=previous.plan,
+                        monthly_price=previous.monthly_price,
+                        row_number=previous.row_number,
+                    )
+                    adjustments.append(
+                        AdjustedRow(
+                            source="subscriptions",
+                            row_number=current.row_number,
+                            record_id=current.customer_id,
+                            field="date_range",
+                            reason="overlapping subscription with same price merged",
+                            original_value=_interval_value(current),
+                            adjusted_value=_interval_value(customer_normalized[-1]),
+                        )
+                    )
+                    current = customer_normalized.pop()
+                else:
+                    adjusted_previous = Subscription(
+                        customer_id=previous.customer_id,
+                        start_date=previous.start_date,
+                        end_date=current.start_date,
+                        plan=previous.plan,
+                        monthly_price=previous.monthly_price,
+                        row_number=previous.row_number,
+                    )
+                    customer_normalized[-1] = adjusted_previous
+                    adjustments.append(
+                        AdjustedRow(
+                            source="subscriptions",
+                            row_number=previous.row_number,
+                            record_id=previous.customer_id,
+                            field="end_date",
+                            reason="overlapping subscription adjusted for later price change",
+                            original_value=_date_value(previous.end_date),
+                            adjusted_value=current.start_date.isoformat(),
+                        )
+                    )
+
+                    if adjusted_previous.end_date <= adjusted_previous.start_date:
+                        customer_normalized.pop()
+                    break
+
+            customer_normalized.append(current)
+
+        normalized.extend(customer_normalized)
+
+    normalized.sort(key=lambda item: (item.customer_id, item.start_date, item.row_number))
+    return normalized, adjustments
+
+
+def _overlaps(left: Subscription, right: Subscription) -> bool:
+    left_end = left.end_date or date.max
+    right_end = right.end_date or date.max
+    return left.start_date < right_end and right.start_date < left_end
+
+
+def _max_end(left: date | None, right: date | None) -> date | None:
+    if left is None or right is None:
+        return None
+    return max(left, right)
+
+
+def _interval_value(subscription: Subscription) -> dict[str, object]:
+    return {
+        "start_date": subscription.start_date.isoformat(),
+        "end_date": _date_value(subscription.end_date),
+        "monthly_price": subscription.monthly_price,
+    }
+
+
+def _date_value(value: date | None) -> str | None:
+    return value.isoformat() if value is not None else None
+
+
 def _row_number(index: object) -> int:
     return int(index) + 2

+def test_overlapping_subscriptions_with_same_price_are_merged(tmp_path) -> None:

Tool Call: apply_patch2026-06-04T09:22:48.178Z
*** Begin Patch
*** Update File: DESIGN.md
@@
 Subscriptions are modeled as half-open intervals: `[start_date, end_date)`. Monthly MRR
 counts the full monthly price for any subscription overlapping a calendar month. Churn is
 counted when a subscription ends and the same customer has no new subscription starting
 within 30 days after the exclusive `end_date`, including the 30th day. Cohort retention
 groups customers by signup month and checks whether they have any subscription active
 exactly three calendar months after signup.
+
+Overlapping subscriptions for the same customer are normalized before metrics are
+calculated. If overlapping rows have the same monthly price, they are merged into one
+continuous interval. If the later row has a different price, it is treated as a price
+change and the earlier interval is shortened to end at the later row's `start_date`.
+Keeping `end_date` exclusive makes this adjustment a clean handoff with no double-counted
+day.
@@
 Invalid row handling favors producing a partial report over failing the whole run. Bad row
 values, duplicate customer IDs, unknown subscription customer IDs, and invalid date ranges
 are rejected and logged; the report is still written from the remaining valid records.
+Subscription overlap fixes are logged as adjusted rows, not rejected rows.
*** End Patch

============================== 10 passed in 0.85s ==============================
Assistant2026-06-04T09:23:21.624Z
Implemented overlap normalization.

Behavior now:
- Same customer, overlapping subscriptions with the same `monthly_price` are merged into one interval.
- Same customer, overlapping subscriptions with a different `monthly_price` are treated as a price change; the earlier interval’s `end_date` is adjusted to the later row’s `start_date`.
- `end_date` remains exclusive, which makes the handoff clean.
- These changes are logged as `adjusted_rows`, not `rejected_rows`.
- JSON now includes an `adjusted_rows` count in `data_quality`.

For the sample CSV, the CLI now reports:

```json
{"adjusted_rows": 3, "rejected_rows": 9, "valid_candidate_rows": 85}
```

Verified:
- `uv run pytest` -> 10 passed
- sample CLI run logs the C004, C017, and C038 overlap merges as adjustments
- touched Markdown/Python files have no lines over 120 chars

Uncommitted changes are present.

User2026-06-04T09:27:51.457Z
now show me how to add a new metric, average revenue per user
Assistant2026-06-04T09:28:12.856Z
To add **average revenue per user (ARPU)** cleanly, add it as another metric function in [src/metrics.py](/Users/dino/work/utilus-saas-data-analytics/src/metrics.py:11), then include it in `build_report`.

For monthly ARPU, use:

```text
ARPU = monthly MRR / number of active customers in that month
```

Because subscriptions are already normalized before metrics run, this should avoid double-counting overlapping subscriptions.

Example implementation:

```python
def monthly_arpu(subscriptions: list[Subscription]) -> list[dict[str, object]]:
    if not subscriptions:
        return []

    first_month = _month_start(min(subscription.start_date for subscription in subscriptions))
    last_month = _latest_relevant_month(subscriptions)
    rows: list[dict[str, object]] = []

    current = first_month
    while current <= last_month:
        next_month = current + relativedelta(months=1)
        active_subscriptions = [
            subscription
            for subscription in subscriptions
            if _overlaps(subscription, current, next_month)
        ]
        mrr = sum(subscription.monthly_price for subscription in active_subscriptions)
        active_customers = {subscription.customer_id for subscription in active_subscriptions}
        arpu = mrr / len(active_customers) if active_customers else 0.0

        rows.append({"month": _month_key(current), "arpu": round(float(arpu), 2)})
        current = next_month

    return rows
```

Then update `build_report`:

```python
def build_report(data: AnalyticsData) -> dict[str, object]:
    return {
        "monthly_churned_customers": monthly_churned_customers(data.subscriptions),
        "monthly_mrr": monthly_mrr(data.subscriptions),
        "monthly_arpu": monthly_arpu(data.subscriptions),
        "signup_cohorts": signup_cohorts(data.customers, data.subscriptions),
        "data_quality": data.data_quality_summary_dict(),
    }
```

Add a focused test in `tests/test_metrics.py`:

```python
def test_monthly_arpu_divides_mrr_by_active_customers() -> None:
    subscriptions = [
        Subscription("C001", date(2024, 1, 1), None, "basic", 30.0, 2),
        Subscription("C002", date(2024, 1, 15), None, "pro", 50.0, 3),
    ]

    assert monthly_arpu(subscriptions) == [
        {"month": "2024-01", "arpu": 40.0}
    ]
```

Also import it:

```python
from src.metrics import monthly_arpu, monthly_churned_customers, monthly_mrr, signup_cohorts
```

That’s the intended extension path: loading/validation remains untouched, and the new metric is isolated in `src/metrics.py`.

-Adding another metric should only require a new function in `src/metrics.py` and adding

+its result to `build_report`. For example, monthly ARPU could be added by calculating
+monthly MRR divided by the number of active customers in that month, using the same
+normalized subscription records that MRR already uses. The loader returns clean typed
+records plus data-quality details, so metric functions do not need to know about CSV
+parsing or Pandera failure formats. If metrics grow, a future improvement would be to
+turn `metrics.py` into a `metrics/` package where each metric lives in its own submodule.
+The JSON report contains compact data-quality counts; detailed rejected-row records are
+logged to stderr or to `--log-file`.

# Design

The project is a small `uv`-managed Python CLI. `main.py` handles command-line arguments
and JSON output, `src/loading.py` loads and validates CSV rows, `src/models.py` contains
typed dataclasses, and `src/metrics.py` contains the business metric calculations.

CSV validation is split into two stages. First, Pandera strict schemas validate the file
shape and coerce scalar values such as dates and prices. Rows that pass Pandera become
valid candidates; rejected rows are retained internally for logging. Second,
application-level checks reject rows that need cross-row context, such as duplicate
customer IDs, subscriptions for unknown customers, and subscriptions whose exclusive
`end_date` is not after `start_date`.

Subscriptions are modeled as half-open intervals: `[start_date, end_date)`. Monthly MRR
counts the full monthly price for any subscription overlapping a calendar month. Churn is
counted when a subscription ends and the same customer has no new subscription starting
within 30 days after the exclusive `end_date`, including the 30th day. Cohort retention
groups customers by signup month and checks whether they have any subscription active
exactly three calendar months after signup.

Overlapping subscriptions for the same customer are normalized before metrics are
calculated. If overlapping rows have the same monthly price, they are merged into one
continuous interval. If the later row has a different price, it is treated as a price
change and the earlier interval is shortened to end at the later row's `start_date`.
Keeping `end_date` exclusive makes this adjustment a clean handoff with no double-counted
day.

Adding another metric should only require a new function in `src/metrics.py` and adding
its result to `build_report`. For example, monthly ARPU could be added by calculating
monthly MRR divided by the number of active customers in that month, using the same
normalized subscription records that MRR already uses. The loader returns clean typed
records plus data-quality details, so metric functions do not need to know about CSV
parsing or Pandera failure formats. If metrics grow, a future improvement would be to
turn `metrics.py` into a `metrics/` package where each metric lives in its own submodule.
The JSON report contains compact data-quality counts; detailed rejected-row records are
logged to stderr or to `--log-file`.

## Failure behavior

Invalid row handling favors producing a partial report over failing the whole run. Bad row
values, duplicate customer IDs, unknown subscription customer IDs, and invalid date ranges
are rejected and logged; the report is still written from the remaining valid records.
Subscription overlap fixes are logged as adjusted rows, not rejected rows.

The CLI exits nonzero only for file-level problems that prevent meaningful validation:
missing files, unparseable CSV input, missing required columns, extra columns, or columns
in the wrong order. Pandera can coerce values inside existing columns, but it cannot infer
or create a missing column, so missing required columns are treated as a fatal file-shape
error rather than a row-level rejection.

*** Add File: DESIGN.md
+# Design
+
+## How the code is structured
+
+The project is a small `uv`-managed Python CLI. `main.py` handles command-line arguments,
+orchestration, JSON output, and optional data-quality logging. `src/loading.py` reads CSV
+files, validates them with strict/coercing Pandera schemas, and applies cross-row cleanup.
+`src/models.py` contains typed dataclasses for customers, subscriptions, rejected rows, and
+adjusted rows. `src/metrics.py` contains the metric calculations and report assembly.
+
+## How the business rules are modeled
+
+Subscriptions are modeled as half-open intervals: `[start_date, end_date)`, so `end_date`
+is exclusive. Monthly MRR sums the full monthly price for every normalized subscription
+that overlaps a calendar month. Churn is counted when a subscription has an `end_date` and
+the customer has no later subscription starting within 30 days after that date, including
+the 30th day. Cohorts are grouped by signup month; 3-month retention checks whether the
+customer has any subscription active exactly three calendar months after signup.
+
+Overlapping subscriptions for the same customer are normalized before metrics run. Same
+price overlaps are merged into one continuous interval. If the later row has a different
+price, it is treated as a price change and the earlier interval is shortened to end at the
+later row's `start_date`.
+
+## How another metric would be added
+
+Add a new function to `src/metrics.py` that takes the already validated and normalized
+records, then add its result to `build_report`. For example, monthly ARPU could be added as
+monthly MRR divided by the number of active customers in that month. The loader and CLI
+would not need to change. If metrics grow, a future improvement would be to turn
+`metrics.py` into a `metrics/` package where each metric lives in its own submodule.
+
+## Assumptions and known trade-offs
+
+Invalid row handling favors a partial report over failing the whole run. Bad row values,
+duplicate customer IDs, unknown subscription customer IDs, and invalid date ranges are
+rejected and logged; overlap fixes are logged as adjusted rows. The JSON report contains
+only compact data-quality counts, while detailed records go to stderr or `--log-file`.
+
+The CLI exits nonzero only for file-level problems that prevent meaningful validation:
+missing files, unparseable CSV input, missing required columns, extra columns, or columns
+in the wrong order. Pandera can coerce values inside existing columns, but it cannot infer
+or create missing columns, so missing required columns are fatal file-shape errors.
*** End Patch
